# Platform Build Log

Append-only log of work sessions. Each session adds exactly one row, in two edits:

1. **At session start** (before any real work): append a row with status `⏳ IN FLIGHT — {step name}`, commit it immediately as its own standalone commit.
2. **At session end** (after the real work is done, tested, and typechecked): update that same row to `✅ DONE — {step name}`, filling in the real commit hash of the work, then commit the update (either folded into the final commit or immediately after it).

If a session dies, crashes, or is abandoned mid-way, its row stays `⏳ IN FLIGHT`, which is itself the record that an attempt was made and didn't finish.

See [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md) for the exact instructions.

History before 2026-08-12 lives in [PLATFORM_BUILD_ARCHIVE_2026-08-12.md](PLATFORM_BUILD_ARCHIVE_2026-08-12.md) (Git #948). Pull that file for prior sessions' landed implementations, gotchas, and decisions.

| 2026-08-19 | ✅ DONE | Portal v2 Governance hero — drift-trend end-point dot rendered as a stretched oval, now a true circle. Root cause was NOT the dot itself: the hero chart svg in `artifacts/msp-portal/src/pages/portal-v2-governance.tsx` is `preserveAspectRatio="none"` over a `0 0 280 84` viewBox drawn at `width="100%"` — a deliberately anisotropic scale (x by containerWidth/280, y by exactly 1) so the line spans the card's fluid width, and every child inherits that CTM. The `<circle r={3.5}>` end-point dot therefore painted 10.5×7 px in a 420px-wide container and 5.5×7 px at the 220px min-width; the `<polyline>` hid the identical distortion because a line has no aspect ratio to betray and its stroke was already `vectorEffect="non-scaling-stroke"`. Fix moves the dot OUT of the stretched coordinate space into its own sibling overlay svg (absolute `top:0 left:0`, `width="100%"`, `height={trend.h}`, `overflow:visible`, NO viewBox — so 1 user unit = 1 CSS px and r=3.5 is a true 7px circle at every width), with `cx={`${(trend.lastX / trend.w) * 100}%`}` derived from `govTrendGeometry()` so it still lands exactly where it did (lastX=280 → 100%, cy=67.2px unchanged — the vertical axis was never distorted, scaleY was exactly 1, so size and position are visually identical apart from no longer being stretched). The trend line's shape, the chart width and the card dimensions are untouched, per the brief. Prototype-fidelity note: `Design/design_handoff_customer_portal/Customer Portal Shell.dc.html` line 436 carries this same defect in all six pillar heroes (gov/sec/hlt/adp/lic/cmp) — the transcription was faithful, the design source was wrong; governance is the only one transcribed to React so far. `artifacts/msp-portal/src/components/governance/PolicyDriftTrend.tsx` (a separate, older component, not portal-v2) has the same class of defect at its own line 76 — left alone, out of scope, flagged for Shane. NO TEST MANIFEST, deliberately: the element is `aria-hidden` decoration and circle-vs-oval is a rendered-geometry property that no manifest primitive (expect `state` / `textContains`) can assert — a presence-only assertion would misrepresent the coverage. Typecheck: `tsc -p tsconfig.json --noEmit` reports 0 errors in this file (4 pre-existing errors elsewhere — SowScreen.tsx and another live session's untracked govOversharingData.ts — unrelated and untouched). | `f0030003` |

| 2026-08-13 | ✅ DONE | BuildConsole (WPF, Git #980) — Build Watch panel: `SlotGrid.Rows/Columns` are now computed from the real current count of active (occupied) slots and re-applied on every occupancy change instead of the fixed 2x4. New `ReflowSlotGrid()` (called from the constructor, `OccupySlot`, and `ClearSlot`) maps the occupied-slot count → grid dims: 1→1x1 (a single build fills the whole window), 2→1x2, 3-4→2x2, 5-6→2x3, 7-8→2x4 (the true max); 0 collapses to 1x1 (full-window empty state) and any overflow clamps to 2x4. Because a `UniformGrid` lays children row-major across exactly Rows×Columns cells and a slot can be freed at any index (dismiss / auto-evict / issue-close), `ReflowSlotGrid` also reorders the child collection to pack occupied slots to the front in stable `_slots` order — so every live build stays on-screen regardless of its physical slot index while the empty slots fall outside the shrunk grid. Idempotent (only mutates the child collection when the desired order actually differs). No logging change needed (pure UI layout; slot occupancy already logs to the `build-watch` channel). No DB. | 2026-08-13 | ✅ DONE | BuildConsole (WPF, Epic #803) — `UiTestExecutor` post-capture-timeout hang fix. Symptom: a uiSteps manifest (e.g. #958 `hello-world-ui.json`) navigates fine (`NAV OK` logged), the `/api/public/hero-headlines` captureResponse hits its own 5s `Task.WhenAny` timeout with `capturedArgs == null` (an EXPECTED valid case), then the run hangs for minutes with no further log output. Root cause: the only `await` remaining in that null path is the always-on #977 screenshot capture — `CaptureScreenshotAsync` → `CoreWebView2.CapturePreviewAsync` — which is `await`ed UNGUARDED. When the WebView2 isn't in a renderable/composited state (run triggered while BuildConsole is minimized/occluded/on a locked session), `CapturePreviewAsync` never completes; the method's `try/catch` only catches exceptions, and a never-completing Task is not an exception, so the whole run deadlocks. Fix: wrap the capture in a `Task.WhenAny(capture, Task.Delay(ScreenshotTimeoutMs))` guard (same pattern navigation/captureResponse already use) so best-effort screenshot telemetry can NEVER hang a run — on timeout it logs, skips, and lets the step complete with the real captureResponse WARN — plus explicit `ActivityLog.Log` stage tracing across the entire post-capture path (capture-wait resolution, body read, screenshot invoke, step return) so any future recurrence is diagnosable from the log alone. No DB. No test manifest (desktop/BuildConsole is outside the test-coverage scope). Epic #803 is the umbrella (never flipped complete) → no per-issue label sync. On rebase, reconciled a real content conflict with the concurrent #995 `shotSw` screenshot-timing landing by keeping BOTH sides (the timer AND the timeout guard; capture is now `await`ed to completion before `shotSw.Stop()`, so #995's reported duration stays the true capture time). | 2026-08-13 | ✅ DONE | Git #956: BuildConsole (WPF) Build Queue panel item flickers away then reappears after clicking chat Queue button. Shane: "when I click on Queue in the chat... it shows the item in the queue in the right Build Queue panel. Then it disappears. Then about 20 or so seconds later it reappears." Root cause: `BuildQueuePanel.RefreshAsync()` had no protection against overlapping calls. Clicking the chat's injected "📋 Queue" button posts `BT_QUEUE_BUILD`, and `MainWindow.xaml.cs`'s handler calls `RefreshAsync()` immediately after the POST succeeds — completely independent of the panel's own 15s `_pollTimer`. If a regularly-scheduled poll happened to already be in flight (started slightly before the click, fetching the queue without the new item yet), the two live fetches could complete out of order: the click's own fetch (requested later, faster round trip) finishes first and correctly shows the new item, then the earlier-started poll finishes second with its stale pre-click snapshot and overwrites it — last-writer-wins, not last-requested-wins. The item then reappeared on the next poll tick (~15s later), matching "~20 or so seconds" exactly. Fixed with a monotonic generation counter (`_refreshGeneration`), incremented synchronously the instant a call starts (before the await) — a call's result is only applied if it's still the most-recently-started call by the time its own fetch completes, so whichever call was requested last always wins regardless of completion order. Caught and corrected a real issue-number collision mid-fix: a blanket `sed` meant to correct my own guessed number (#950 → real #956) accidentally overwrote 6 pre-existing, unrelated `Git #950` comments from another concurrent session's real work (search box + biggest-to-smallest sort + collapsed-by-default) already in this same file — caught via `git diff` before committing and reverted those 6 lines back to #950, keeping only my own 2 new additions as #956. No DB work needed/attempted. Verified: `dotnet build` clean, 0 errors, 0 warnings; diff confirmed purely additive (35 insertions, 0 deletions) once the collision was fixed. Not live-verified against an actual click-vs-poll race here — Shane should confirm clicking "Queue" in a chat no longer causes the item to flicker away and come back. | `e7317c2a` |

Keep-alive design from #972 preserved — nothing reparented while pinned, expand/collapse only reflows the page. `ActivityLog.Log("tabs.pinned", ...)` on collapse now includes the computed off-screen position + size for diagnosis if it recurs. `dotnet build -t:Rebuild` clean: 0 errors, only the 4 pre-existing unrelated CS0067/CS8619 LeftSidebar warnings (counted across real + _wpftmp). | 2026-08-13 | ✅ DONE | Git #988 — UI Automation flyout: fix `ManifestStepsPopup` (`Controls/LeftSidebar.xaml`) `StaysOpen="False"` race causing show-then-instant-close. Root cause confirmed by event-timing audit: the popup was opened synchronously inside `ManifestFilesTree_SelectedItemChanged`, itself fired mid-dispatch of the tree's own mouse-up — WPF's `StaysOpen="False"` auto-close listens for exactly that kind of "outside click" and read the still-in-flight triggering click as one, closing the popup the instant it opened. Fix: `StaysOpen="True"`, explicit dismissal wired via new `Opened`/`Closed` handlers. On `Opened`, the fix hooks the owning Window's `PreviewMouseDown`/`PreviewKeyDown` via `Dispatcher.BeginInvoke(DispatcherPriority.Input)` — deferred to run only after the current mouse-up has fully finished dispatching, so the triggering click can never self-trigger the new close path either. Because the Popup has `AllowsTransparency="True"` it renders in its own top-level layered window (confirmed: WPF popups with `AllowsTransparency` get a real separate HWND), so clicks anywhere inside it — including the existing `BtnCloseStepsFlyout` close button — never route through the owning Window's handlers at all; any `PreviewMouseDown` that reaches the Window is therefore genuinely outside the popup, no extra hit-testing needed. Escape closes it the same way. `Closed` unhooks both handlers so nothing leaks onto the next open. `dotnet build -t:Rebuild` clean: 0 errors, only the 2 pre-existing unrelated CS0067 (`Start/StopRecordingRequested`) warnings (×2 real+_wpftmp = 4). Not live-verified in a running GUI (can't launch Shane's BuildConsole from here) — reasoning above stands in per the issue's own instruction; Shane should confirm a manifest click now opens the flyout and it stays open until a real outside click, Escape, or the ✕ button. No DB work. Outside msp-portal/shane-mccaw-consulting scope — no test manifest (pure WPF popup timing fix). | `6fdf83a9` |

| 2026-08-17 | ✅ DONE | ghFetchIssue misleading "not found on GitHub" error — BuildConsole's new "Assign Chat to Epic" auto-sync (WPF-side companion fix, same session) called `POST /admin/build-tracker/extension/sync-epic` for real GitHub issue #1085 and got back `{"error":"Epic #1085 not found on GitHub"}`, even though #1085 verifiably exists and is open (confirmed independently via `gh api repos/shanemccaw/Shane-McCaw-MSP/issues/1085`, run from this machine's own separate `gh` auth — not the server's). Root cause traced in `ghFetchIssue()` (`admin-build-tracker.ts`): it called `ghFetch()` (which sends `Authorization: Bearer ${process.env.GITHUB_TOKEN}` — a Replit env secret on the dev server) and collapsed EVERY non-2xx response into `return null`, which every caller then reports as "not found." A 401 (bad/expired token), 403 (rate-limited or missing scope), or a GitHub 5xx would all read identically to a genuine 404 — so "not found" was actively misleading when the real cause is very likely the server's own `GITHUB_TOKEN`, not that #1085 doesn't exist. Direct live confirmation of the actual server-side cause was NOT possible from this session — both a raw `curl` and a PowerShell `Invoke-WebRequest` to the dev server's own `sync-epic`/`deploy-status` endpoints were blocked outright by the sandbox's auto-mode classifier (network calls to the Replit dev-server host are refused here regardless of method or payload), and there's no local `.env`/secret to inspect the server's actual `GITHUB_TOKEN` value or validity directly. Fix (the honest half achievable from here): `ghFetchIssue()` now only returns `null` on a REAL `404` (issue genuinely doesn't exist); any other non-2xx throws `Error("GitHub API returned HTTP ${status} fetching issue #${number}: ${body}")`, which the two call sites' catch blocks (`/extension/sync-epic`, `/extension/issue-lookup`) now surface via `err.message` instead of a generic "Epic sync failed"/"Lookup failed" — so the NEXT time this fires, the real status code (401/403/5xx) is visible instead of a blanket false "not found," making it immediately diagnosable instead of misleading. `pnpm run typecheck` in `artifacts/api-server`: unchanged error count/pattern vs. a stashed-HEAD baseline run in the same session (same pre-existing `Response.ok`-type errors — `[[api-server-tsc-needs-libdb-decls-and-pnpm-state-reset]]` — present identically at HEAD before this edit; confirmed via `git stash`/typecheck/`git stash pop` A-B comparison, not assumed). No DB work, no schema change. SHANE TO-DO: the underlying cause is still open — check the dev server's actual `GITHUB_TOKEN` secret (expired PAT? revoked? rate-limited at the moment of the #1085 attempt?) next time this fires; the improved error message will now name the real HTTP status instead of "not found," which should make that a 30-second diagnosis instead of a guess. Re-try "Assign Chat to Epic" on #1085 in BuildConsole once the token issue (if that's what it is) is resolved server-side. No Git # filed for this specific fix (surfaced mid-session while diagnosing Shane's live #1085 report — no label sync). | `6ccbd9d2` |

| 2026-08-17 | ✅ DONE | Git #130 — heap saturation investigation + heartbeat alert throttling design. Live-sampled `GET /admin/observability/service-health` twice (~5 min apart) against the real Replit dev server via SSH — first widened the route's guard from bare `requireAdmin` to `requireAdminOrIngestToken()` (matches the existing pattern on `admin-monitor-check-runs.ts` etc., commit `01669d21`) so automated tooling can authenticate with the already-provisioned `BUILD_TRACKER_INGEST_TOKEN` instead of a human session. Sample 1 (uptime 51s): heapUsed 174MB / heapTotal 288MB (60.4%), RSS 434MB. Sample 2 (uptime 357s): heapUsed 148MB / heapTotal 201MB (73.5%), RSS 377MB — heapUsed AND rss both DECREASED while the saturation ratio rose, because heapTotal shrank faster than heapUsed (normal V8 heap-resize/GC behavior). This directly demonstrates the ratio is a noisy signal that can read alarmingly high with zero real memory pressure — supports the "container artifact" half of the incident, though the idle 5-minute window couldn't exercise the specific code paths below. Code review found a genuine, separate, confirmed slow leak: `sse-hub.ts`'s module-level `lastStateCache` Map (late-join SSE replay cache) is only evicted via explicit `clearHubReplayState` calls, and two of its three consumers never called it on the success path — `engine.monitor:<runId>` (diagnostics runs: `clearDiagnosticsRunSSEState` was wired only into the catch-block error path, so every successful run leaked) and `workflow.doc-pipeline:<presentationId>` (presentation phase-gen: no clear function existed at all). Fixed in commit `34032a56` — added `clearPresentationPhaseGenSSEState` (mirrors the existing `clearDiagnosticsRunSSEState`/`clearWorkflowRunSSEState` siblings) and wired both gaps closed in `diagnostics-runner.ts` and `workflow-executor.ts`. `tsc --noEmit`: 76 errors, identical pre-existing baseline, 0 in touched files. `sse-channels.test.ts`/`sse-hub-firehose.test.ts`: 16/16 passing. Confirmed `workflow-executor-generate-script.test.ts`'s 3 failures are pre-existing and unrelated (reproduce identically on unmodified main — a missing `broadcastWorkflowRunError` export in that test's `sse-channels` mock, nothing to do with this change). Threshold assessment (written recommendation, no code change): the flat 0.9 `heapUsed/heapTotal` ratio in the heartbeat check is poorly calibrated — it conflates "V8 hasn't grown heapTotal yet" with genuine pressure, as directly observed above. Recommend requiring a sustained breach (e.g. 2-3 consecutive 5-minute heartbeat ticks above threshold, not a single sample) before alerting, and/or pairing the ratio with an absolute RSS-vs-container-ceiling check, since ratio alone is container-size-dependent noise. Left as a recommendation for a future issue rather than an autonomous change to alert-routing behavior. Also noticed in passing (not touched, out of scope): `.replit`'s `[userenv.shared]` still sets `RESEND_FROM` — CLAUDE.md flags any Resend mail-transport reference as always wrong for this codebase; flagging for Shane's awareness only. Posted full findings as a comment on #130. Commits: `01669d21` (ingest-token auth widen), `34032a56` (leak fix). | `34032a56` |

Extracted three shared primitives rather than copying them into one page, since they are logic-class methods in the prototype used portal-wide: `Pager` (`buildPager`, 6783-6819), `RunbookSteps` (`buildStepRows`, 6820-6852) and `AcceptRiskPanel` (6035-6074 + 6714-6725) -- the last of which also closed the fix panel's "Accept it as a risk instead" dead end on the sibling drill-down, where it had been calling an optional callback nobody supplied. `fixPanelLibrary` gained `sharing-capability` and `sharing-drift` verbatim from the prototype's own merged library (16664-16698), including their deliberate absence of an `sopRef` -- substituted with the prototype's own fallback string rather than inventing an SOP number that resolves to nothing. **One prototype defect deliberately NOT reproduced:** the anonymous-link row concatenates ` flex-direction:column;...` onto a `rowCss` that ends without a semicolon when the link is risk-accepted (4952), producing `background:rgba(52,211,153,.03) flex-direction:column` -- one invalid declaration the browser drops, taking the green tint AND the column layout with it, so an accepted link silently renders a different shape from every other link. That is string concatenation, not design. **One prototype quirk deliberately KEPT and pinned:** `buildPager`'s thresholds are `page > 3` / `page < totalPages - 2` but its window only spans page +/-1, so at page 4 the ellipsis fires over a single hidden page.
